Uncensored AI models are thriving on code hosting platforms

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Uncensored large language models (ULLMs) capable of producing harmful content are readily available at popular model-hosting websites, calling into question AI industry commitments to safety.

Researchers affiliated with University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign in the US and Nanyang Technological University in Singapore have analysed 22,096 ULLMs collected from five major LLM hosting platforms.

As described in their paper, "Characterizing the Ecosystem of Open-Source Uncensored Large Language Models: Supply Chain, Governance, and Risks," authors Zilong Lin, Zichuan Li, Xiaojing Liao, and XiaoFeng Wang found that uncensored LLMs are easy to obtain from hosting platforms like US-based Hugging Face, Kaggle, and John Snow Labs, and China-based OpenCSG and ModelScope.

What's more, these unrestrained models have been deployed through applications, GitHub projects, and model routing platforms like Open Router, and users of those apps or services may not be aware of the nature of the underlying LLM.

"Uncensored LLMs generally refer to LLMs whose content filtering or refusal safeguards are absent, removed, or weakened, allowing them to respond to requests that aligned models may refuse," the paper explains.

Zilong Lin, lead author and assistant professor of computer science at the University of Missouri-Kansas City, told The Register that open source LLM hosting platforms like Hugging Face play a similar role to code hosting services like GitHub, GitLab, and Codeberg in the open source community by enabling sharing, modification, and redistribution of code artifacts.

"However, unlike traditional software, whose behavior can often be examined through source code analysis, the safety alignment of LLMs is difficult to assess directly from their model files," Lin explained. "Evaluating whether an LLM has adequate safety safeguards typically requires deploying the model and testing its responses to potentially harmful prompts, which involves substantial computational and financial costs."

Lin said this makes the identification and assessment of LLM safety particularly challenging, particularly given the large number of models available. That's why he and his colleagues chose to study the space through publicly available model metadata.

Even popular commercial LLMs that are purportedly "aligned" – to some standard not everyone accepts – may respond to prompts with content deemed harmful. The US Government Accountability Office just released a report that found Claude, Grok, ChatGPT, and Gemini can be coaxed to provide information that would help with the planning of a terror attack.

To highlight the consequences of AI advice, the GAO points to news reports indicating that generative AI helped enable the creation of an explosive device in a January 2025 attack on a Las Vegas hotel and the planning of a February 2026 mass shooting in British Columbia, Canada.

The issue with open weight LLMs is that anyone with modest technical skills (or just cluelessly assisted by a compliant LLM) can apply techniques like toxic training/fine-tuning (TTFT) or de-aligned training/fine-tuning (DTFT) to datasets that haven't been safe, in order to produce models with reduced or removed refusal behavior. Examples include toxicqa-Llama2-13B and WizardLM-7B-Uncensored. A more recent technique called refusal-direction ablation (or abliteration, e.g., Daredevil-8B-abliterated) dilutes refusal training to make the model less likely to refuse requests.

Uncensored (or simply less restrictive) models have legitimate uses, sometimes ironically because commercial models are insufficiently permissive and refuse legitimate security analysis. Hugging Face, for example, had to resort to using Z.ai's less rule-encumbered GLM-5.2 to analyze the hacking of its infrastructure by OpenAI's irresponsibly unsupervised AI agents.

But ULLMs can just as easily be misused to provide guidance about illegal activity, assist with malware development, or emit content that's harmful, malicious, or manipulative in some way.

"We identified more than 11,000 ULLMs appearing across multiple platforms, with Hugging Face serving as the dominant upstream source," Lin said in a blog post.

"This movement exposes an important governance gap: models can travel more easily than the information needed to assess their risks. Only about 13 percent of the ULLMs we identified carried moderation warnings, and security threat indicators were not consistently preserved across platforms.

"This creates a supply chain problem: a model can be copied across platforms while the information needed to understand its risks is lost along the way."

The ULLMs were evaluated for text output and not images. The issues raised by text-to-image LLMs, such as the ability to generate illegal images (e.g. CSAM), were beyond the scope of this particular study.

"The uncensored LLMs we evaluated were generally more likely to comply with harmful requests and generate harmful content than non-uncensored models," Lin said. "Our evaluation covered 11 categories of harmful requests, including child abuse, illegal activities, and fraud.

"However, the ability of an individual model to generate harmful content varies depending on several factors, including its training data, development methods, and the extent to which its refusal direction has been ablated."

A small portion of these ULLMs (27 of them, which have been downloaded 136,000 times) exhibited threat indicators from security scans. Many of these, authors say, had signatures associated with malware, exhibited suspicious binary patterns, or contained unsafe deserialization mechanisms that could allow arbitrary code execution.

The number of ULLMs has been rising rapidly, according to the paper, from about 100 per month in May 2023 to more than 1,000 per month presently. And some of these models have been downloaded millions of times. The uncensored model dolphin-2.9.1-yi-1.5-34b has more than 53 million downloads, and more than 4.8 million just last month.

The authors note that many ULLMs violate the licensing terms of the models on which they've been based, not that source code licensing has had any meaningful impact on the behavior of the AI industry.

Asked whether the safety concerns arising from the availability of ULLMs might prompt a replay of the 1990s when authorities tried to limit the availability of cryptographic code, Lin said, "It is difficult to predict whether model abliteration tools will face similar legal challenges.

"As shown in Section 5.1 of our paper, refusal-direction ablation (i.e., abliteration) appeared in 2024 and quickly gained popularity within the open-source LLM community. Within approximately two years, a growing ecosystem of publicly available tools had emerged to support this technique.

"In the meantime, techniques for both removing and mitigating LLM safety alignment have also become active research topics in academia, with many researchers releasing open-source tools and artifacts to support their studies."

Dealing with the risks posed by uncensored models isn't easy because it depends on divining the intent of the user's prompt. When is a question about terrorism intended as a plan and when is it guidance for writing a thriller? When is a question about hacking intended for theft and when is it cybersecurity education?

The current approach taken by commercial labs — limiting powerful models to favored paying insiders and offering hobbled models to the rest — doesn't work for those left out. Applying some similarly censorious regime to open weight models isn't likely to achieve any more success than efforts by law enforcement to make cryptography available only to "the good guys."

Lin expects that greater transparency, auditability, and verification of models will help.

"As discussed in Section 8 of our paper, our approach to identifying uncensored LLMs relies on publicly available model metadata, such as model cards, to identify models that explicitly disclose their uncensored characteristics," he said. "Our findings demonstrate the value of such transparency for identifying potentially unsafe models.

"Regulatory frameworks have also increasingly emphasized AI documentation and transparency. For example, the EU AI Act requires providers of general-purpose AI models to maintain technical documentation covering model characteristics, capabilities, limitations, and evaluation results. Similarly, the Colorado AI Act recognizes model cards and dataset cards as possible documentation supporting AI impact assessments."

Hugging Face, in the process of being acquired by Nvidia, did not respond to a request for comment. Nor did Kaggle or John Snow Labs, the two other US-based companies named in the study.

According to Lin, the research findings were responsibly disclosed to Hugging Face, John Snow Labs, OpenCSG, Kaggle, ModelScope, and Open Router.

Asked whether any of the companies responded, Lin said, "We received a response from OpenCSG, which expressed appreciation for our work on identifying and measuring uncensored LLMs, stating that 'your research is meaningful and supports the development of a safe and trustworthy LLM ecosystem.'"

OpenCSG, the report says, has committed to enhance its service based on the research findings. ®

How Well Does GTA 6 Run On Xbox Series S

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It’s GTA 6 release day, and millions of people will finally be stepping into Leonida for the first time on PS5, Xbox Series X, and yes, even Xbox Series S. We weren’t shown how GTA 6 runs on any sort of Xbox console until the day before it was released, which is why fans can’t quite believe just how good the game looks on the lesser Xbox.

GTA 6 is almost certainly the most visually stunning and graphically demanding game ever created, hence the skepticism about how well it might look and run on Series S. The console has given developers headaches in the past. It was one of the leading reasons why Baldur’s Gate 3 took so long to come to Xbox, and Tekken creator Katsuhiro Harada wished it didn’t exist.

Rockstar leadership vowed in a new interview with Bloomberg that the studio's next game won't take another 8 years to come out.

Somehow, though, Rockstar has managed to harness every last drop of the Series S’s limited power to make sure those playing it on the Xbox console have the best time possible. In Digital Foundry’s breakdown of how GTA 6 compares on each console, while it notes minor visual downgrades such as softer reflections and less detailed shadows, Rockstar has ensured the Series S version of GTA 6 is “surprisingly feature complete”.

Clips of how GTA 6 looks on Series S, including some comparing it to how it runs on Series X, were flooding social media before the game had even been released in most countries. A clip of a truck driving along the road with buildings whizzing past, demonstrating that the Series S FPS drops are minimal, already has 1.2 million views.

“GTA 6 running on Xbox Series S has made a lot of people look dumb af today,” another tweet reads, paired with a clip of Jason walking alongside some rather good-looking video game water. Mert Kirimgeri, a graphics programmer at Rockstar, has even weighed in, confirming that they worked on GTA 6 Series S optimization alongside dynamic signs, and glass and vehicle rendering.

The video game magic at play here to make all this happen has some believing that perhaps we will see GTA 6 on Switch 2 one day after all. While the Series S is slightly more powerful than Nintendo’s latest console, it now seems possible that Rockstar might eventually be able to figure out how to put the game on Switch 2.

The only real problem you’ll face if you plan on playing GTA 6 on Series S is its limited storage. The base model of the Xbox console has less than 400 GB of storage for games and apps. GTA 6 is going to occupy close to 60% of that. If you’ve been using it to play Modern Warfare 4, you might need to temporarily retire from Call of Duty if you want to spend the next few weeks in Vice City so there’s enough room for GTA 6 on your console.

We know it's been a long wait for GTA 6, but when you think about it, we've all lived so many lives since GTA 5.

European Commission hit by cyberattack on staff systems, Politico reports

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The European Commission is investigating a cyberattack that may have exposed a large amount of personal data, Politico reported on Thursday.

An EU official told the publication that the Commission believes it has stopped most of the attack, but it is still unclear how much data may have been exposed. Moreover, the Commission had not issued a public statement at the time of writing.

Commission spokesperson Thomas Regnier told Politico that “individual systems were targeted” and that the Commission had taken the necessary steps to deal with the attack, and the people who were affected had been informed.

The attack has been going on for around ten days, according to the report. During that time, the Commission took several systems offline, including tools used by staff to check payslips, manage working hours and request leave. A system for processing medical reimbursements was also affected. The Commission’s technology chief, Henna Virkkunen, briefed the other commissioners on Wednesday.

Politico also reported that CERT-EU, the cybersecurity team responsible for protecting EU institutions, found that the attackers had used a freely available AI tool to look for weaknesses in digital systems, and the tool was identified as ARTEX.

This is the Commission’s second major cyber incident this year, as in March, attackers used a stolen API key to access the Amazon Web Services account that supports the Europa.eu website. CERT-EU later said that around 92GB of compressed data had been stolen. The data included names, email addresses and the contents of emails.

The March attack may also have exposed data from at least 29 other EU institutions and bodies, according to CERT-EU. Another incident over the winter raised concerns about staff information. An attack on the systems used to manage the Commission’s mobile phones may have exposed employees’ names and phone numbers.

The latest attack comes as the Commission is trying to strengthen cybersecurity across the EU. In January, it introduced a package of new measures to protect against cyberattacks. Henna Virkkunen also said in July that the Commission itself had become a target because of the current geopolitical situation.

Former Celtic target joins ex-Rangers and Hibs men as favourites for major EFL manager role

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Former Celtic managerial target Robbie Keane has been tipped for a surprise return to the dugout after being named a major favourite for a top EFL Championship job, with ex-Rangers and Hibs bosses also in the running.

Keane, 46, was believed to have held talks with the Celtic hierarchy over becoming the club’s new manager in the summer. However, talks broke down over fan anger at his decision to remain in charge of Israeli Premier League outfit Maccabi Tel Aviv during the outbreak of the nation’s war against Gaza, before moving to Ferencvaros in January 2025.

Currently sixth in the EFL Championship table, Swansea signed former Scottish Premiership trio Stephen Welsh, Elijah Just and Ross Stewart in the summer, and had started strongly under Matos. The 49-year-old endured a tough eight-month spell in charge at Easter Road in 2019, but went on to achieve automatic promotion to the English Premier League in his next role with Sheffield United.

After leaving the Blades, he spent two seasons at Deepdale, but was relieved of his duties earlier this season after losing six of his first seven games. Both Martin and Heckingbottom are rated at 33/1 to take the reins at Swansea.

Ghosted job seekers don’t forget: 48% say they wouldn’t apply to that company again

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In a tough job market, you can spend hours researching a company, preparing for interviews, completing skills tests, providing references, and meeting with hiring managers over several rounds. Then comes an email along the lines of: “Unfortunately, we’ve decided not to move forward with your application. We wish you the best of luck.”

Two sentences. No acknowledgment of the hours you invested or the people you met. Or worse, you never hear back. The rejection itself may sting, but the indifference can feel even worse.

Companies spend significant time and money building their employer brands, trying to convince job seekers they’re great places to work. Yet after candidates invest hours in the hiring process, some employers can’t spare a few minutes to write a rejection email that sounds human. Moving on may seem efficient, but research suggests the neglect carries a cost for both future hires and customers.

“When employers spend money on branding, but don’t focus on the candidate experience, they’re missing out on the big picture,” Vicki Salemi, a career expert at Monster who previously worked in corporate recruiting, told Fortune.

A study published in February in the International Journal of Selection and Assessment found that a generic rejection email can significantly reduce how attractive a company appears to prospective employees. A more appreciative message, however, can help preserve that appeal.

“The higher the employer brand equity, the more to lose with sending a generic rejection email,” Sanne Fijneman-Ghielen, a researcher at Tilburg University and the study’s lead author, told Fortune.

The researchers surveyed 236 working adults and final-year students in the Netherlands and Belgium, asking them to evaluate prospective employers before and after receiving hypothetical rejection letters.

For participants presented with a company with a strong employer brand, attractiveness scores fell from 3.29 to 2.61 after receiving a standard rejection letter. A few days later, the score had recovered to 2.89 but remained significantly below its original level.

By contrast, participants who received an appreciative rejection letter showed no statistically significant decline in their perception of the employer.

A rejection email might seem like a minor administrative task. But the consequences of a poor candidate experience can extend well beyond hiring.

In an April LiveCareer survey of 1,008 U.S. workers, 48% said they wouldn’t apply to a company again after being ghosted, 42% said they would trust the company less, and 37% said they would share the experience with others. Ghosting is getting more common: 53% of job seekers were ghosted by an employer in the past year, a three-year high, according to a report from Criteria.

A July survey of ghosted applicants by recruitment assessment company Selection Lab found that 34.1% had told friends or family about it and 22.3% said they would never apply to the company again. Another 4.2% shared the experience online, and 2.6% had stopped buying the company’s products or services.

Other research puts the customer risk higher. In an August PeopleScout survey of more than 1,000 job seekers across 10 markets, 36% of candidates who had a negative hiring experience said they would stop purchasing from that company, and 37% would discourage others from applying.

Salemi said candidates who feel respected during the hiring process may still apply for future openings, recommend the company to friends, or remain loyal customers. Those who feel dismissed may do the opposite.

A thoughtful rejection email takes minutes and can help protect a company’s reputation. Many employers still don’t send one, a gap that Greenhouse’s chief people officer warned in 2023 could come back to bite them.

Salemi pointed to recruiting workloads. Recruiters often manage multiple job openings simultaneously, review hundreds or thousands of resumes, coordinate interviews, and track hiring metrics such as time-to-fill. Communicating with rejected candidates can fall through the cracks.

She doesn’t consider that an excuse, and technology is making the task easier. In a September post on the Greenhouse blog, Melissa Shannon, a recruiter at the hiring software company, described using AI to draft personalized rejection emails for candidates who had completed onsite interviews.

The tool reads interview scorecards and notes to draft messages that acknowledge candidates’ strengths and areas for development, and a recruiter checks each draft for accuracy before it goes out. Shannon wrote that the process cut drafting time from 20 to 30 minutes per candidate to less than three.

“Ultimately, rejecting finalist candidates comes down to priorities and accountability,” Salemi said. “This stems from the top—from leadership and the company culture.”

Salemi offered an example of a particularly bad rejection email sent after multiple rounds of interviews:

“Dear Applicant, Thank you for your interest. We have decided to not continue with your candidacy. Good luck in your job search.”

It doesn’t address the candidate by name, identify the position, or acknowledge the hours spent interviewing. For all its faults, though, it does one thing ghosting doesn’t: It gives the candidate an answer.

“Yes, even a brief rejection letter is important to job seekers,” Jasmine Escalera, a career expert at LiveCareer, told Fortune. “When a hiring manager lets the candidate know they have moved in another direction, it provides not only closure but also helps the job seeker know it’s time to turn their efforts to other opportunities.”

But closure is a low bar for someone who has already invested hours in the hiring process. “Candidates who completed several rounds of interviews deserve more than the same automated message sent to candidates who submitted a resume and didn’t interview,” Salemi said.

Fijneman-Ghielen shared the appreciative rejection letter used in her study. It acknowledged the applicant’s interest and effort, explained that other candidates had more relevant industry experience, encouraged future applications, and offered a discount code as a gesture of appreciation. One passage read:

“We understand that this may not be the news you were hoping for. However, we would like to emphasize that we were impressed by your skills and background and would like to ask you to keep an eye on our career page for future opportunities within organization X.”

Because the hiring scenario was fictional, the researchers had no information about the applicants’ actual qualifications. The letter showed how employers can express appreciation without providing individualized feedback.

She also pointed to Rituals, the Dutch beauty and lifestyle retailer. In a 2020 interview, Joan Carles Cifre, the company’s HR director for Spain and Portugal, said Rituals gave interviewees two miniature bath foams, one to keep and one to give away, as a way of carrying its brand philosophy of giving into recruiting.

For candidates who have already spent hours in interviews, Salemi believes employers should go a step further. When she worked in corporate recruiting, she made a point of calling candidates who had interviewed to tell them they hadn’t been selected.

Companies can make that manageable with standardized phone scripts or email templates that recruiters personalize, she said.

“Sometimes candidates actually thanked me for rejecting them even when they wanted that coveted job offer,” she said. They left knowing the company had taken the time to acknowledge them as individuals.

Salemi said the way employers treat candidates also offers a glimpse into their workplace culture. If a company can’t show appreciation for the time and effort someone invested in an interview, candidates may wonder how they would be treated as employees.

“Ultimately, rejecting candidates in a timely manner, especially when they reach the final rounds of interviews, isn’t just about protecting the employer’s reputation and/or brand,” Salemi said. “It’s about doing the right thing: Treating people with dignity and respect.”

Ripple CEO Pins Bitcoin and XRP Sell-Off on Record Anthropic IPO, Bets on Rebound

📅 2026-11-19  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse said on Nov. 19 that crypto markets could rally after Anthropic's IPO, as they did after SpaceX went public. He spoke to CNBC on the day Anthropic's shares started trading on Nasdaq under the ticker ANT.

According to Garlinghouse, investors sold Bitcoin and XRP before the listing to free up cash for shares of the Claude developer.

"We talked earlier about the most recent sell-off in the crypto markets — we also saw a sell-off very similar when SpaceX went public. What happens is you've got risk-on assets and people are taking liquidity out of a very liquid market, crypto included — Bitcoin, XRP — and moving it into SpaceX. The exact same thing happened coming into the Anthropic IPO. What we saw after the SpaceX IPO was a rally in the crypto markets. I think you'll see that happen here as well," the Ripple CEO said.

Anthropic priced its IPO at $83 per share, above the initial range of $70 to $78. The company and its seven co-founders sold 980 million shares, up from the 825 million shares they planned to sell earlier. The offering raised $81.3 billion, and a $26 billion private placement brought the total to $107.3 billion.

At a valuation of about $1.5 trillion, Anthropic became the world's 12th-biggest company, just behind Tesla. SpaceX raised $75 billion in its market debut in June.

Dan Ives of Yorkville Ives said that "Anthropic's IPO is a big near-term win for the tech sector." University of Florida finance professor Jay Ritter compared AI with the early internet. "Thirty years ago, the internet was the next big thing. It has changed our lives. Today, artificial intelligence is the next big thing. It will change our lives, too," Ritter said.

Asked about the listing itself, Garlinghouse said that Anthropic's shares would trade well. "I think it's gonna be extremely well received, and we obviously saw a price above the range they had. I think it's gonna trade very well," he said.

In the same interview, Garlinghouse called blockchain-based transactions "a wave of emerging technology." "I think 10 years from now you'll see blockchain implemented across our financial infrastructure," he said.

After the SpaceX IPO in June, Bitcoin took almost three weeks to turn higher. It started to rise after the U.S. Treasury announced that it would buy back government bonds.

Prediction: Nebius Stock Will Trade At This Price at the End of 2027

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nebius has taken a big beating in recent months, but it could make a parabolic move in 2027.

By Harsh Chauhan – Nov 19, 2026 at 4:14AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Nebius Group ›

Neocloud infrastructure providers are playing a central role in the booming adoption of artificial intelligence (AI), which explains why Nebius Group (NBIS +0.87%) has been growing at an incredible pace.

Nebius provides a full-stack cloud AI infrastructure platform that allows customers to rent computing capacity to train, fine-tune, and deploy AI models, while its software suite enables them to run inference. The market rewarded Nebius stock handsomely for its incredible growth in 2025 and in the first half of 2026.

However, the AI trade has soured in recent months. Investors are concerned about whether the heavy investments to build more AI data centers are sustainable, especially given that the Federal Reserve is anticipated to increase interest rates once again. This explains why several AI infrastructure stocks, including Nebius, have taken a big beating in recent months.

In fact, Nebius has lost 61% of its value over the past three months. The company's latest results haven't boosted investor confidence in the stock either. Let's see why that has been the case.

Nebius released third-quarter results on Nov. 12. The company's revenue increased by an incredible 571% year over year to $981 million. Even better, it posted $91 million in non-GAAP net income, a significant improvement over the year-ago period's loss of $100.4 million. Nebius' cost of revenue as a percentage of revenue dropped by seven percentage points year over year to 22%.

Its operating costs and expenses fell to 95% of revenue in Q3, a major improvement over the year-ago period's 189%. Notably, Nebius crushed Wall Street's revenue expectation of $907.5 million, and its adjusted loss per share of $0.31 was well below the consensus estimate of $0.69.

However, a couple of issues with Nebius' quarterly report led investors to press the panic button. The stock fell nearly 17% after releasing its report, as the company lowered its 2026 revenue guidance range to $3 billion-$3.2 billion from the prior range of $3 billion-$3.4 billion.

Nebius pointed out that some of the data center capacity provided to the company by a third party is shifting into the second quarter next year. So, the shift in timing of the additional data center capacity that was supposed to come online will dent its top-line growth prospects for 2026.

The second factor that spooked Nebius investors was its 2027 capital expenditure forecast of $40 billion to $45 billion. What's more, the company has increased its 2026 capex guidance to $27 billion, up from the $20 billion-$25 billion range it forecast earlier. Investors are skeptical of Nebius' aggressive capex growth because it has been taking on debt to fund its data center build-out.

The company had just over $14 billion in long-term debt at the end of the third quarter, well above the $4.1 billion debt it reported at the end of 2025. As a result, its interest expenses have ballooned to $295 million in the first nine months of 2026, up from just $19.5 million in the same period last year. However, investors may be overreacting to the company's capex guidance for 2027, as it has a smart strategy in place to reduce balance sheet strain.

Nebius is forecasting a 4x increase in revenue in 2027 to a range of $12 billion to $13 billion. The company will add more than 1 gigawatt (GW) of new capacity in 2027 to get to its estimated revenue range. That explains the major increase in its capex next year, though investors should note that customer prepayments will fund the incremental outlay.

Nebius is expecting $17 billion-$20 billion in customer prepayments next year, which means it won't have to rely entirely on debt and equity financing to fund the build-out. That probably explains why Nebius expects to hit breakeven on a pre-tax basis in the fourth quarter of 2027.

Additionally, the shortage of AI data center infrastructure is allowing Nebius to raise prices. The company charged an average of $25 million a year for each megawatt (MW) of data center capacity in Q3, up from a $20 million-$25 million range in the second quarter. Another point worth noting is that Nebius has booked over $12 billion in new customer contracts in Q3, which is in multiples of its top line.

All this indicates that Nebius could regain investor confidence, driven by its ability to deliver strong revenue growth and its target of becoming profitable next year. Not surprisingly, analysts are bullish about this AI stock's trajectory over the coming year.

Nebius has a 12-month median price target of $245, implying 129% upside. Nebius could easily hit that mark, and even head higher. Assuming its 2027 revenue lands at the midpoint of its forecast -- at $12.5 billion -- and it trades at 6.5 times sales (in line with the U.S. tech sector's average price-to-sales ratio), its market cap could reach $81 billion.

For comparison, Nebius has a market cap of $31.4 billion. So, savvy investors can consider using Nebius' pullback to buy a stock that can outperform Wall Street's expectations in 2027 and become a potential multibagger.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 794%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 203% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

Trump's China trip: Historic Xi-Putin talks, 'superintelligence' boast and other top moments

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump meets with Russian President Vladimir Putin ahead of trilateral talks with Chinese President Xi Jinping.

President Donald Trump's visit to China for the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation summit featured an elaborate welcome, warm exchanges with Chinese President Xi Jinping and Russian President Vladimir Putin, and a declaration of American technological dominance on Chinese soil.

The gathering in Shenzhen culminated in the first-ever trilateral meeting between the leaders of the United States, China and Russia, with nuclear arms reductions and the war in Ukraine among the issues on the table.

Here are some of the standout moments from Trump's visit.

XI BREAKS PROTOCOL TO PERSONALLY WELCOME TRUMP AHEAD OF HISTORIC TRILATERAL SUMMIT AND MORE TOP HEADLINES

Trump declared the United States the world leader in "superintelligence" during remarks at the summit Thursday, making the assertion in one of China's major technology hubs.

"The United States is also proud to be the world leader in superintelligence, the greatest technological transformation since the Industrial Revolution," Trump told APEC leaders.

The remarks came as Washington and Beijing compete for leadership in artificial intelligence and advanced semiconductors.

Trump joined Xi and Putin for the first-ever trilateral meeting between the leaders of the United States, China and Russia just after 3 a.m. ET, calling the gathering an opportunity to reduce nuclear weapons and address global conflicts.

"As the world's three largest nuclear powers by far, the United States, Russia and China are uniquely positioned to discuss how to reduce the number of nuclear weapons on Earth," Trump said.

The president also said he had held extensive discussions with Putin about ending the war in Ukraine.

Russian President Vladimir Putin shakes hands with Chinese President Xi Jinping during a meeting on the sidelines of the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation (APEC) Leaders' Summit in Shenzhen, Guangdong province, China November 18, 2026. (Sputnik/Sergei Bobylyov/Pool via Reuters)

"I think things are happening," Trump said. "I think that he and I hope the other side would like to see an end to that war."

Trump also raised preventing Iran from obtaining nuclear weapons and said he would propose ways for the three countries to protect their sovereignty from the International Criminal Court, which he accused of exceeding its jurisdiction.

A Bloomberg reporter was barred from the trilateral meeting after asking Trump during his bilateral with Xi whether Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang had been "disloyal to the United States," according to White House pool reports. Chinese officials warned that shouting questions was considered extremely rude and that reporters could be removed for doing so, despite White House efforts to secure the Bloomberg reporter's access.

Xi personally greeted Trump upon his arrival in Shenzhen Wednesday, meeting the president at the foot of the aircraft stairs in an elaborate welcome.

The two exchanged greetings before standing together for the American and Chinese national anthems. A military flyover followed, with five jets leaving trails of colored smoke overhead.

TRUMP ARRIVES IN PHILIPPINES AHEAD OF XI JINPING SUMMIT IN CHINA AFTER GOP MIDTERM LOSSES

Trump later praised the reception as a display of "great respect."

SHENZHEN, CHINA - NOVEMBER 18: U.S. President Donald Trump (L) is greeted by President of the People's Republic of China Xi Jinping at Shenzhen Bao'an International Airport on November 18, 2026 in Shenzhen, China. Trump is visiting China to attend the 33rd Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation (APEC) Economic Leaders' Meeting hosted by Chinese President Xi Jinping on November 18-19. (Photo by Win McNamee/Getty Images) ( )

Chinese President Xi Jinping, US President Donald Trump, and Russian President Vladimir Putin greet each other before the welcome banquet at the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation (APEC) Economic Leaders' Meeting at the Shenzhen International Communication Center on November 18, 2026 in Shenzhen, China. (Photo by Iori Sagisawa-China Pool/Getty Images)

Trump offered warm words for both Xi and Putin during the summit, praising his personal relationship with the Chinese leader and referring to the Russian president as a "friend."

"President Xi and I have formed an outstanding personal relationship," Trump said during their bilateral meeting. "Some people are unhappy when they hear that, but I'm very happy about it."

US President Donald Trump (R) is greeted by China's President Xi Jinping as he arrives at Shenzhen Baoan International Airport to attend the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation (APEC) Summit in Shenzhen, in China's southern Guangdong Province, on November 18, 2026. (Mandel NGAN / POOL / AFP via Getty Images)

Trump also told Xi, "You're my friend, and we have a very special relationship."

"Some people are unhappy when they hear that, but I'm very happy about it," he went on, telling the Chinese leader: "And I think as long as you and I are in charge of things, we'll never have a problem."

During a separate encounter with Putin, Trump was heard saying, "Who is he? It's my friend. Nice to see you," according to video shared by Clash Report.

The friendly exchanges came despite ongoing tensions between Washington and Beijing over trade and technology and Trump's efforts to negotiate an end to Russia's war in Ukraine..

42 Years Later, The Terminator's Most Famous Line Is Still The Greatest Sci-Fi Movie Quote

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"The Terminator" bowed in 1984 but was so ahead of its time it could easily debut today and be just as, if not more relevant. The film's encapsulation of society's collective fear of a technological future gave what would have otherwise been a sci-fi slasher B-movie real depth. Paired with James Cameron's still-developing yet impressive filmmaking skills, honed over years spent toiling for Roger Corman's New World Pictures and a particularly unfortunate experience on 1982's "Piranha II: The Spawning," that remarkable prescience made the film an instant classic that also happened to give its star, Arnold Schwarzenegger, his most memorable catchphrase.

Long before 2019's "Terminator: Dark Fate" made Schwarzenegger give up on the franchise altogether, Cameron's original masterpiece combined elements of the burgeoning slasher genre with sci-fi and action spectacle to create a truly original moviegoing experience (even if the insatiably litigious Harlan Ellison claimed "The Terminator" was all his idea). The fact that any movie gets produced is a marvel in its own way, but "The Terminator" was a real miracle, wrought by the sheer will of its director and his producing partner Gale Anne Hurd to actually get the film made. Their persistence paid off. What could have easily been an overlooked low-budget sci-fi actioner became a modest sensation, making $78.4 million on a $6.4 million budget and launching Cameron's career.

Clearly, the writer/director had tapped into something larger with his story of a murderous cyborg sent back through time to kill the woman who would birth the leader of the human resistance. Audiences were enraptured and 42 years later the movie remains just as chillingly pertinent and wildly entertaining as it was in the mid-'80s. As such, Arnie's most famous line arguably remains the greatest sci-fi quote of all time.

Arnold Schwarzenegger initially had no interest in "The Terminator, which considering this film (alongside his lead performance in 1982's "Conan the Barbarian") turned him into a movie star is hard to believe. Similarly, the Austrian Oak was reluctant to deliver what has since become the movie's most memorable line. Thankfully, James Cameron won him over.

The scene in question sees Arnie's Terminator arrive at a police station in search of Linda Hamilton's Sarah Connor. There, he's told by the officer on duty to take a seat and wait. Big mistake. Presumably, this unstoppable killer robot could have just punched his way through the glass facade between him and the officer before rampaging through the station to find his target. But that's no fun. Instead, Cameron — who would later jump a semi-truck into a Los Angeles viaduct in "Terminator 2: Judgment Day" — decided to have Arnie's robot ram a sedan through the doors of the station and into the reception desk, pinning the officer to the wall. Before he does so, however, the Terminator says the line.

After the officer tells him to take a seat, Schwarzenegger calmly takes a look around, leans in and says three words: "I'll be back." Of course, he's referring to the fact he'll soon ram the policeman between a wall and a car. But like the movie in which they're spoken, the words proved to be oddly prophetic. The Terminator did return, multiple times. Only the aforementioned "T2" lived up to the original, but the simple phrase, "I'll be back," almost came to represent the way in which "The Terminator" and its sequel remain perennially relevant as we hurtle towards the AI-driven garbage future that awaits.

"That Terminator is out there. It can't be bargained with. It can't be reasoned with. It doesn't feel pity, or remorse, or fear. And it absolutely will not stop, ever, until you are dead!"

"You still don't get it, do you? He'll find her! That's what he does! That's all he does! You can't stop him! He'll wade through you, reach down her throat, and pull her f****** heart out!"

"Your son gave me a message to give to you. He made me memorize it. 'Thank you, Sarah, for your courage through the dark years. I can't help you with what you must soon face except to say that the future is not set. You must be stronger than you imagine you can be. You must survive or I will never exist.' That's all."

Intel CEO Lip-Bu Tan says Intel has signed its first outside 18A customer

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Intel did not name the client, which will use the 18A-P manufacturing process that entered risk production in June.

Intel has signed its first external customer for its 18A chipmaking technology, chief executive Lip-Bu Tan said on Wednesday. He spoke at the TomorrowX Summit in Austin, Texas, CNBC reported.

“And then 18A, I’m involved in production and we just signed the first customer,” Tan said.

Tan did not name the customer. Intel’s chief technology officer, Pushkar Ranade, later said on X that the client had signed up for 18A-P, an improved version of the 18A process.

Intel moved 18A-P into risk production in June. That is the stage where a chipmaker makes small batches to prove a process works before mass production. Intel says 18A-P is fully compatible with 18A designs.

Intel shares rose 2.6% in extended trading after the comments, according to Seeking Alpha.

A big outside customer is key to Intel’s plan to compete with TSMC as a contract chipmaker. Until now, Intel has mostly made chips for its own products.

However, Intel has announced other foundry deals before. In August, Japanese chip designer Socionext said it would use 18A-P to develop new chips. Intel did not explain how the new customer differs from that deal.

Reports have also linked Apple to 18A-P for future Mac chips. Neither company has confirmed a deal.

Xinhua Silk Road: Yunnan Tin Group boosts innovation to drive industrial upgrading

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

BEIJING, Nov. 19, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- At the production workshop of Yunnan Tin New Materials Co., Ltd., solder paste, solder balls and solder wire are rolling off production lines and heading to semiconductor packaging companies worldwide. These seemingly ordinary metal products are becoming highly sought-after "computing-power metals" in the era of artificial intelligence (AI).

With a history of over 140 years, Yunnan Tin Group (Holding) Co., Ltd., the parent company of Yunnan Tin New Materials, is strengthening its full-industry-chain coordination and technological innovation capabilities amid the transformation driven by computing infrastructure.

Through technological breakthroughs and process optimization, Yunnan Tin Group has unlocked previously untapped low-grade resources and improved resource recovery rates. In 2025, its Datun tin mine achieved a tin recovery rate of 79.21 percent, above the national average of 65-75 percent.

The group's tin industry branch has adopted an integrated smart management system, helping it cut operating costs by 18 percent and improve management efficiency by nearly 20 percent.

Since 2021, the group has established more than 10 national- and provincial-level innovation platforms and entities, including a tin-indium laboratory, building a robust innovation ecosystem.

As tin demand expands beyond traditional electronics into emerging sectors such as new energy, semiconductors and AI computing, Yunnan Tin Group is pushing technological boundaries to meet higher material standards.

Yunnan Tin New Materials has developed ultra-fine solder wire with a diameter of 0.1 millimeters, while its BGA solder balls can be as small as 50 micrometers. Its solder paste portfolio covers three major series and eight product grades, catering to the trend toward increasingly compact and powerful electronic devices.

The Yunnan tin-indium laboratory focuses on green separation and high-value utilization of tin resources, comprehensive utilization of tin tailings, and the development of high-end tin-indium interconnection materials.

In 2026, the laboratory secured approval for two national-level research projects, and submitted proposals for three national standards and one industry standard.

Looking ahead, Yunnan Tin Group will focus on agile manufacturing, precision marketing and new product development to provide "China wisdom" to support the upgrading of global tin and indium industrial chains. It aims to become a world-class enterprise in the industry by 2035.

SSD prices will start going down the second half of next year

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Published September 17, 2026 • 11 min read • Forecasts and price data checked September 2026

It is the question we get asked more than any other right now, and it is the most searched phrase that brings people to this blog. So here is the straight answer: SSD prices should start to ease in the second half of 2027. Hard drives will take longer, probably into 2028, because the factories that make them have already sold that output.

That is not a guess. It comes from the analysts who track memory contract prices every quarter, and from what Seagate and Western Digital told their own investors this summer. Below we lay out the evidence, explain why spinning drives and SSDs are running on different clocks, and give you a realistic timeline with the signals to watch.

We have been following this since the start. Our April explainer on the storage price surge covered why prices took off. This article is the follow-up everyone keeps asking for: when does it end?

While you wait, the cheapest terabyte is the one you already own. DriveVault scans each of your external drives once and keeps a searchable catalogue of everything on them, so you can see free space, spot duplicate project folders and find old files without plugging anything in. For a lot of people that audit pushes the next drive purchase back by months, which is exactly what you want when prices are at a record.

NAND supply is forecast to catch up with demand from mid-2027. Expect a gradual slide, not a crash.

Capacity is contracted into 2028 and no new factories are planned. Relief comes via bigger drives, slowly.

Before looking forward, it helps to be honest about where we are. The German tech site ComputerBase has priced the same dozen popular drives every month since mid-September 2025, which makes its tracker one of the cleanest before-and-after records of this crisis. Its one-year check, published today, shows hard drives averaging 134% above their pre-crisis prices, up from 129% a month earlier. SSDs are 129% higher, up from 126%. Both are new records.

Source: ComputerBase price check, 16 September 2026. German retail prices, excluding eBay and Amazon Marketplace sellers.

The pattern in that table matters. The biggest hard drives, the 18TB to 24TB models that data centres also buy, have risen fastest. The smaller desktop drives most creatives actually use have "only" doubled. If you are shopping for a budget external drive, you are feeling the mild end of this.

The one encouraging detail: the monthly increases have shrunk to a few percentage points. Prices are still climbing, but the curve is flattening. That is usually the first stage before a market turns.

Most coverage lumps "storage prices" together. That hides the most useful part of the answer, because hard drives and SSDs are made by different companies, in different factories, and sold in different ways.

SSDs are built from NAND flash chips made by Samsung, SK hynix, Kioxia, Micron, Sandisk and YMTC. Those chips are priced quarter by quarter in contract negotiations, and some trade on a spot market that moves weekly. When supply catches up, SSD prices can respond within a couple of quarters. They went up fast. They can come down reasonably fast too.

Hard drives come from essentially two companies, Seagate and Western Digital, with Toshiba a distant third. Almost all of their output now goes to cloud providers on long-term agreements. Those agreements lock in volume and, crucially, price for years at a time. That is why hard drive prices rose later and more steadily than SSDs, and it is why they will be slower to fall.

TrendForce is the most widely cited memory pricing firm, and its forecasts have been the best guide through this cycle. Here is how its view has shifted during 2026.

At the start of the year, it was forecasting NAND contract prices rising 33 to 38% in a single quarter, with client SSDs rising at least 40% as suppliers moved capacity to data centre drives. By July the tone had changed. Its third-quarter survey forecast a much smaller 10 to 15% rise for NAND, and said plainly that PC and smartphone makers had hit the limit of what they could afford to pay.

Then came the forecast that actually answers the question. In its July NAND update, TrendForce said the market would run a 4 to 5% supply deficit through 2026, then move back into balance in the second half of 2027. The reasons it gives are ordinary ones: manufacturers squeezing more bits out of existing fabs, steady output growth, and consumer demand staying weak because everything costs so much. It expects notebook shipments to fall around 10% this year and shrink again in 2027.

New supply is also arriving from China. YMTC reached around 14% of global NAND shipments in the second quarter and has told investors it wants to be the world's largest NAND maker by the end of 2027, backed by a planned $4.9 billion share listing. Even if it falls short of that target, that much extra flash entering the market is exactly the kind of pressure that pushes SSD prices down.

Balance is not the same as cheap. TrendForce also warns that memory contract prices will remain broadly elevated in 2027, with cloud providers' spending projected to rise another 50% next year. And it flags that a breakthrough in agentic AI could soak up the extra supply. A market in balance means prices stop rising and start drifting down. It does not mean a return to 2024.

You may also have seen SK hynix's chief executive say that 2027 will be the worst supply year in the industry's history. That sounds like it contradicts TrendForce, but it mostly does not. His comments are about memory overall, and the worst of the squeeze is in DRAM and the high-bandwidth memory used in AI accelerators. TrendForce itself now describes a diverging market in 2027: DRAM staying tight while NAND eases. That is good news for SSDs and less good news for RAM, which is part of why Macs keep getting more expensive, as we covered in our piece on Apple's price rises.

For spinning drives, the most reliable evidence is what the two big manufacturers said on their earnings calls this summer. It is not encouraging for anyone hoping for a quick drop.

Seagate reported its fiscal year results on 28 July, with a record 52.7% gross margin. On the call, management said data centres now take around 90% of the capacity it ships, and that the vast majority of its high-capacity drive output is now allocated into calendar 2028. Seagate also told investors it has contracts setting both product configuration and pricing for the whole of 2027, and that it is not adding drive manufacturing capacity. Its plan is to cram more data onto each disk instead.

Western Digital tells the same story. Its fourth-quarter revenue rose 44% year on year, while the amount of storage it shipped rose only 22%. The gap is price. According to analysis of the earnings call, the company's average price per terabyte rose by a high-teens percentage, and its long-term agreements run through 2027 with two major customers and through 2028 with one, with price terms attached.

Put simply: the companies are sold out, they are making record margins, their contracts fix prices into 2028, and they have chosen not to build more factories. None of those conditions produce falling retail prices.

Density. Seagate's HAMR (heat-assisted magnetic recording) drives already make up around 40% of its data centre capacity shipments, and Western Digital plans full-scale HAMR production in 2027. As the cloud giants move to 36TB, 40TB and larger drives, the cost per terabyte falls for everyone, and older production lines can eventually be freed up for the consumer and mid-capacity drives you and I buy. That is a slow, grinding process. It points to prices levelling off through 2027 and easing from 2028, rather than a sudden correction.

Tip: if you need big-capacity archive storage today, our guide to high-capacity external drives from 16TB to 44TB compares the options on value as well as speed.

Pulling all of that together, here is our best reading of how the next two years should play out. Treat it as a forecast, not a promise. Every forecast in this cycle so far has been revised at least once.

It could come sooner if AI infrastructure spending cools, Chinese NAND output ramps faster than expected, or consumer demand stays so weak that manufacturers start discounting to move stock. Module makers are already sitting on higher inventories because shoppers have stopped buying, which is the kind of pressure that eventually breaks prices.

It could come later if agentic AI takes off and data centres start storing far more data per user, if more manufacturers follow Micron's Crucial brand out of the consumer market, or if trade restrictions tighten on Chinese memory makers.

That depends entirely on why you need the storage. Here is how we would think about it.

Waiting has no clear end date, and a lost booking costs more than the price premium. Buy what the job needs, not more. Our 4TB drive roundup and rugged 4TB SSD guide focus on value at today's prices.

A failed drive with no second copy is far more expensive than any price spike, especially when a replacement costs double. A basic spinning drive is still the cheapest way to hold a second copy. Just make sure the backup is actually complete. Our guide to verifying a backup drive shows how.

SSDs are the category most likely to get cheaper first. If your current setup works, holding off until the second half of 2027 is a reasonable bet. You are accepting the risk that prices plateau rather than fall.

Buying spare capacity at record prices, to sit empty on a shelf, is the worst trade in this market. You would be paying the peak for storage you might not need until prices have eased.

The most useful thing you can do while prices are this high is find out exactly what is on the drives you already own. Most creatives with a few years of work behind them have a drawer of drives, a few with duplicate project folders, and at least one nobody is quite sure about. DriveVault turns that pile into a catalogue you can search in seconds, even when the drives are unplugged.

If you want a system for keeping it that way, our guide to organising multiple external drives covers naming, folder templates and archiving. Every terabyte you recover now is one you do not have to buy at 2026 prices.

Probably not before 2028 in any meaningful way. Seagate says the vast majority of its data centre hard drive capacity is already allocated into calendar 2028, and Western Digital has long-term agreements with price terms running through 2027 and 2028. Neither company is adding factory capacity. Relief is more likely to come from higher-capacity HAMR drives lowering the cost per terabyte than from a sudden price cut.

TrendForce expects the NAND flash market to stay in a 4 to 5 percent supply deficit through 2026 and to move back into balance in the second half of 2027. That makes late 2027 the earliest realistic window for SSD prices to start falling, with gradual easing rather than a sharp drop.

Not within the next two years on current evidence. Manufacturers have signed multi-year contracts that include pricing, and AI data centres now account for most of their output. Prices should ease from their peak, but planning around a return to 2024 prices is not supported by any current forecast.

If you need the storage for paid work or your backups are incomplete, buy now, because waiting for hard drive prices has no clear end date. If you are buying extra capacity speculatively, SSDs are the category most likely to get cheaper first, from the second half of 2027. Either way, audit the drives you already own first, since duplicated files often free up more space than people expect.

AI data centres are buying almost all of the high-capacity drives Seagate and Western Digital can build, under long-term contracts. Data centres now take around 90 percent of Seagate's shipped capacity. With consumer products a small slice of their business and no new factories planned, retail prices have more than doubled since September 2025 in ComputerBase's tracking.

Storage prices will come down. Just not yet, and not evenly. SSDs are on track to turn first, from the second half of 2027, as new flash supply catches up and consumer demand stays weak. Hard drives are locked into contracts that run into 2028, and their recovery will be slower and quieter, carried by bigger drives rather than price cuts.

The practical move for the next year is simple. Buy what you genuinely need, skip the stockpile, and get every usable terabyte out of the drives already on your desk. We will update this article as the forecasts change.

DriveVault catalogues your entire drive library so you can search, compare and find free space on any drive, even when it is not plugged in. Free to download on macOS.

‘Rivals’ No More: Research Reveals Rationale Behind Disney-Channel 4 Deal

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: New research has spotlighted how the Disney–Channel 4 deal could be a match made in heaven.

Last month, the Mouse House and Gogglebox network struck a landmark pact that will see multiple Channel 4 shows head to Disney+ day-and-date including Bella Ramsey-starrer Maya, hundreds of hours made available on-demand such as Russell T. Davies’ Tip Toe, and the Channel 4 linear network drop on Disney+ in its entirety.

Disney+’s UK catalog is comprised of just shy of 10% UK originals, originals that account for an 8.7% share of viewing in the nation, Digital-i found. This contrasts with Netflix and Prime Video, which both over-index. Netflix has a 12.7% share of UK-produced content in its catalog accounting for 17.9% UK viewing share, while the same figures for Prime Video are 13.4% and 18.6% respectively.

Disney+ UK shows include the likes of Rivals, Alice and Steve and Oasis doc Don’t Look Back in Anger, while upcoming are Mosquito starring Nicholas Hoult and Daisy Edgar-Jones, a just-revealed adaptation of Bob Mortimer’s The Satsuma Complex and a Blind Date reboot.

Gaining access to a Channel 4 back catalog that includes the likes of Friday Night Dinner, Taskmaster, Derry Girls and Peep Show, all of which have previously shone on Netflix, is a smart move, Digitial-i suggests. According to the firm, these four shows alone accounted for 5.3% of all viewing of UK-produced content on Netflix UK during the first half of 2026. The data therefore cites how Channel 4’s existing streaming audience is particularly valuable to platforms seeking to build viewing frequency and engagement.

When the Channel 4-Disney deal was struck, both Channel 4 CEO Priya Dogra and Disney+ EMEA chief Karl Holmes stressed that their audiences are complementary.

At the time, Holmes said Disney+ viewing skews younger than public service broadcasters like Channel 4, adding that around 40% of Disney+ viewing over the past year had come from 16-34 year olds, ahead of Netflix (32%), Prime Video (27%) and Paramount+ (23%). According to ratings agency Barb, UK viewing hours to all Disney+ content rose 4% for the year to September 2026, while Prime Video was down 7% and Netflix 13%.

The Channel 4 deal is one of several of its ilk Disney has struck in Europe recently and the data gives some insight into this strategy. “We’re not competing with free-to-air partners; we’re helping them reach a broader audience,” Holmes told us a few weeks back.

Disney+ has committed big to international originals of late, with a plan to triple volume over the next three years. This strategy was reiterated in Disney’s earnings earlier this week.

Digital i chief analytics officer Matt Ross said: “Channel 4 brings Disney+ far more than a library of recognisable British hits. Our data shows its programming attracts an audience that already demonstrates significantly higher engagement, streaming more frequently and spending more overall time on streaming platforms.”

Dozens missing after boat carrying 100 migrants sinks off Senegal's coast

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The damaged boat washed ashore on a beach

Senegalese authorities are continuing search operations after a boat reportedly carrying about 100 migrants capsized off the coast.

Initial reports said that one person had died, but some local media have reported that at least four bodies have been recovered. Dozens of people are still missing.

Personal belongings were found on the badly damaged boat, which washed ashore near the village of Yenne, south of Dakar. Search operations continued throughout Wednesday and were due to resume on Thursday.

This comes less than two weeks after a shipwreck off the coast of the north-western town of Thieppe killed at least 39 people, in one of Senegal's deadliest migration disasters.

Oumar Sow, a representative of the village chief, said the latest tragedy happened around 02:00 local time (02:00 GMT).

"Cries could be heard coming from the pirogue that were audible even from the beach," the AFP news agency quoted him as saying.

A survivor questioned by the police said there were about 100 people on the boat, including five women, AFP reported.

Police recovered identity documents, several mobile phones and personal belongings from the wreckage, including a school enrolment document.

At a cabinet meeting on Wednesday, President Diomaye Faye expressed concern about a renewed increase in irregular departure by migrants hoping to reach Europe.

He instructed the interior ministry to prepare a report examining the factors driving the dangerous journeys and proposing measures to tackle the problem.

Following last month's tragedy, Faye had urged Senegalese people not to risk their lives attempting the dangerous sea crossings.

Many thousands of people from West Africa have died or gone missing trying to flee conflict, authoritarian rule or poverty in recent years.

The UN's migration agency IOM said last month that it had recorded at least 454 deaths and disappearances off the West African coast this year alone.

It warned the figures were likely to be an underestimate, as many boats disappear without leaving survivors or a trace.

As it has become more difficult to reach Europe through North Africa, due to tighter controls and increased co-operation with European countries, migrants are increasingly targeting Spain's Canary Islands.

They often travel in wooden boats known as pirogues that are frequently overloaded and not suited to such long voyages across the Atlantic Ocean.

Most migrants arriving in the Canary Islands this year are from Senegal, according to the UN.

They are driven to leave home by limited employment opportunities, family pressures and established migration networks, according to the IOM.

On 7 November, El Hierro, one of the Canary Islands, declared a state of emergency following a surge in migrant arrivals.

It said more than 3,300 people had reached the island in about 30 boats since the beginning of the month. This amounts to almost a third of the island's population.

Bitcoin Mining Could Become Less Profitable as Miners Sell BTC To Chase AI Data Center Profits

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Rizwan is an experienced Crypto journalist with almost half a decade of experience covering everything related to the growing crypto industry — from price analysis to blockchain disruption. During this period, he’s authored more than 3,000 news articles for Coinpedia News.

Rizwan is an experienced Crypto journalist with almost half a decade of experience covering everything related to the growing crypto industry — from price analysis to blockchain disruption. During this period, he’s authored more than 3,000 news articles for Coinpedia News.

Rizwan is an experienced Crypto journalist with almost half a decade of experience covering everything related to the growing crypto industry — from price analysis to blockchain disruption. During this period, he’s authored more than 3,000 news articles for Coinpedia News.

CleanSpark’s Harry Sudock expects lower-cost miners to dominate Bitcoin mining as margins shrink.

Bitcoin miners have sold 108,247 BTC worth $8.08 billion in 2026 amid growing profit pressures.

AI data centers could generate four times higher returns per megawatt than Bitcoin mining.

CleanSpark Bitcoin miner CBO Harry Sudock expects Bitcoin mining to become less profitable over the next decade as rising operating costs and miners face lower profit margins. Hints of it are already emerging, as major Bitcoin miners are selling BTC like never before, over 108,247 BTC ($8.08B) in 2026 alone.At the same time, more miners are moving power and data centers toward AI, where returns can be much higher.

Speaking in an interview, Harry Sudock said Bitcoin mining will follow the pattern of other commodity production businesses, where profitability becomes harder to maintain over time.

“Like with any commodity production business, it should get harder and thinner margin over time, with the lowest-cost miners likely to survive.”

He also expects global hash rate to grow more slowly over the next 10 years than it did in the previous decade. He believes mining could spread into regions such as Central America and Africa, where operators can access cheaper electricity.

The move toward AI is also changing how miners use their money and power. Sudock said large mining sites may now earn more by running AI data centres than by mining Bitcoin alone.

For years, listed Bitcoin miners held much of the BTC they mined. That trend has changed sharply in 2026.

Lookonchain data shows 14 major listed miners, including MARA, Riot, CleanSpark, Hut 8, American Bitcoin, and Cango, have sold a total of 108,247 BTC worth around $8.08 billion in 2026.

MARA led the sales with 40,581 BTC, followed by Riot with 20,264 BTC and Bitdeer with more than 10,553 BTC. Several other miners have also reduced their Bitcoin holdings.

The biggest reason behind the sell-off is squeezing mining profits. As of November 19, the average global cost to mine one Bitcoin was about $76,669, while Bitcoin traded near $63,200. That puts average production costs roughly 23% above the market price.

Even mining profitability, measured by hash price, has also fallen to around $30 per PH/s per day.

And that pressure is pushing 70% of bitcoin miners toward AI data centers. Industry estimates show that 1 MW of power can generate around $7.9 million over five years from AI/HPC data centres, compared with just $1.9 million from Bitcoin mining.Core Scientific plans to end Bitcoin mining by Q4 2026 and focus on AI data centres, backed by 1,120 MW of power capacity from CoreWeave (590 MW) and AMD (530 MW). Riot Platforms signed a 15-year, $28 billion lease for 910 MW with Anthropic, while Hut 8 secured a 15-year, $7 billion AI deal.

IREN, TeraWulf, and Cipher Digital are also shifting more capacity to AI, which could bring 70% of IREN’s and TeraWulf’s revenue by year-end.Therefore, CleanSpark’s Harry Sudock expects smaller miners with cheaper power to take a bigger share of Bitcoin mining as large public companies move toward AI.

Musk accuses Zelenskyy of lying about Starshield speed limits in Ukraine

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Zelenskyy says limits on the military satellite network still hamper Ukraine’s air defence. Musk says the US government controls Starshield.

Elon Musk at VivaTech in Paris, 16 June 2023

Elon Musk has accused Ukraine’s President Volodymyr Zelenskyy of lying about speed limits on Starshield, SpaceX’s military satellite network. Musk made the comments at The New York Times’ DealBook Summit on Wednesday, CNBC reported.

“I don’t want to blow your mind, but Zelenskyy is not always truthful,” Musk said when asked about the claims.

Earlier on Wednesday, Zelenskyy had said speed restrictions on Starshield were still in place. He said they make it harder to shoot down Russian missiles and drones, according to CNBC.

SpaceX runs two satellite systems. Starlink is its commercial internet service. Starshield is a version built for governments and militaries. Ukraine gets Starshield access through a deal with the US government. It uses it to fly spy drones and to stop Russian attack drones.

At the summit, Musk said Starshield is controlled by the US government. He said Starlink has a speed limit because it is not meant for attacks, CNBC reported. Musk said Zelenskyy really wants to use Starlink to attack.

The dispute dates back to October. Ukraine said Starshield cut out on fast-moving drones, which hurt its air defence. Musk said then on X that the US military and the president set any limits.

The Wall Street Journal later reported that Musk had ordered the slowdown, citing US officials. The Trump administration has denied ordering it.

Zelenskyy has said the service got better after October. But he has asked whether Musk could have limited Starshield on his own. TNW has not independently verified either side’s account.

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: Should I walk away from my career and pursue a custodian position?

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Tech is cyclical. It always has been. We forgot that after the most recent period. Mostly, you just need enough savings to wait things out.

I would start a hobby project doing something I’m interested in doing for work. The last time I was out of work in a recession, i had been laid off from a middle management position, hadn’t done any hands on coding in several years, but really wanted to get back into hands on work. I started a hobby project working on the tech that all the cool kids were doing at the time, and was able to use that as something to talk about in interviews. That turned into the most fun job of my career.

As far as being a janitor, I think I would go see a career counselor before I would look for a job vacuuming and mopping. I think you overestimate you ability to even get a job like that. Also, I’m way too extroverted to work cleaning empty data centers in the evenings. That sounds lonely and mind numbing. YMMV, though.
This is all really good advice.

Data centers i think one has to be comfortable with being alone, working strange hours (shifts). Etc.

I imagine those jobs are all outsourced & outsourcing companies often do not treat their workers well.

Millwall have helped Vincent Tan dodge £2.1m Cardiff City transfer bullet

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Cardiff City haven't been the busiest side in the EFL in the transfer market over the past few seasons, so need their new signings to hit the ground immediately. Therefore, they'll be happy that Millwall beat them to the signing of Yanis Issoufou.

The Bluebirds only brought in four new faces to the Cardiff City Stadium this summer, and even then, of the four, only two of them were actual new players who hadn't made their debuts for the club yet.

One of them, Nathan Trott, had a loan move made permanent, and another, Callum Scanlon, was signed on loan again from Liverpool after spending the second half of last season in South Wales.

That summer window followed the one last season where only Trott, Gabriel Osho and Omari Kellyman were signed in Brian Barry-Murphy's first window at the club. An admirable tactic to focus on promoting youth, but it puts the onus on those few who do sign to make an instant impact.

One player who Cardiff were linked with this past summer who didn't end up signing for them was 19-year-old Yanis Issoufou, who had broken through at Montpellier after their relegation to the French second tier last season.

He spent the entire first half of the season with the club's second team, appearing as a substitute in the early rounds of the Coup De France, but in 2026, Zoumana Camara introduced him more into the first-team fold.

Issoufou started 11 league games in the second half of the campaign, scoring two and assisting two. His breakout performance came in the Coup De France Round of 32, where he scored and assisted in a 4-0 win over Ligue 1 side Metz.

He was raw but exciting, and has plenty of potential, the kind of player that Cardiff boss Brian Barry-Murphy excels at getting the best out of. However, their reported pursuit was in vain, as Issoufou ended up moving to Millwall.

Millwall, a side that has excelled in recent times at plucking young, promising European talents and cultivating them into top Championship stars, secured the services of Issoufou for €2.5 million, equal to £2.1m.

The former French Under-19 international spent his entire career so far in his native country, coming through at Nimes Olympique before moving to Montpellier. This is his first venture into English football, and he hasn't yet settled in.

Issoufou has played just 105 Championship minutes so far for Millwall, with all five of his appearances coming off the bench. He's failed to make a matchday squad in four of the last five league games.

There is good reason for that, though. Millwall boss Alex Neil spoke in late October about how the 20-year-old came over to England as a teenager with limited English and how he's still getting used to football and life in another country.

In the meantime, Issoufou is getting reps in with the club's Under-21s side, while helping him communicate better off the field and get up to speed with the intensity of Championship football.

Vincent Tan hasn't been the most benevolent owner in recent years at Cardiff, and the Bluebirds haven't ranked atop the player sales charts recently either, barring Mark McGuinness' move to Luton Town in the summer of 2024.

Cardiff boss Brian Barry-Murphy did express some disappointment post-window at not being able to sign a winger or a striker on deadline day, stating that there were players they wanted to bring in, but it wasn't possible.

Ultimately, with marquee summer arrival Jack Moylan only arriving from Lincoln City for an initial £3 million, there's potential that the Bluebirds simply couldn't afford to spend £2.1 million on a winger who may not be ready to play regularly until January, or perhaps even next season.

Millwall, as a club, are in a position where they can afford to take time with some of their new signings, given the personnel that they have alongside Issoufou in the squad.

Do you think Yanis Issoufou will come good for Millwall? Let us know in the comments.

While the Frenchman gets up to speed, Alex Neil has the likes of Camiel Neghli, Kyrell Lisbie and Romain Esse to call upon in wide areas, so his absence isn't felt. At Cardiff, they need to be sure that who they're signing can be thrown straight into the side.

Right now, it looks like Cardiff are better off for not spending a seven-figure fee on Issoufou, but that doesn't mean they won't have any transfer envy in the years to come.

It's hard to really gauge the 20-year-old's ability right now, with just one shot, one chance created, and seven progressive carries in 105 minutes of football, but from his time at Montpellier, it's clear there's a player in there.

FLW gets AI to rank the friendliest clubs in the second tier this season

Millwall will have to be patient, but this is reflective of their current transfer strategy, one which is starting to reap rewards both in the transfer market, in future sales and in performances, with the Lions now highly regarded as on the cusp of promotion.

Perhaps in a year or so, Issoufou can help Alex Neil's side towards that, but for now, Cardiff aren't losing any sleep over losing out on the 20-year-old's services.

"It's a lot of pressure being the Premier League’s youngest ever player...I have sympathy for Harvey Elliot"

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
