Japan bond yields surge to 3.75%, hiding a quiet Bitcoin trap

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Demand for Japan’s 20-year bonds fell sharply as yields rose, putting yen-funded Bitcoin trades under scrutiny.

Demand fell at Japan’s Nov. 18 auction of a 20-year government bond, with investors offering to buy 3.64 times the amount the Finance Ministry accepted in the competitive auction, putting pressure on traders who borrow yen to hold Bitcoin.

The average accepted yield rose, while the yen barely moved between the Bank of Japan’s two 5 p.m. Tokyo readings.

In the Nov. 18 results, the ministry recorded ¥1.914 trillion in competitive tenders for issue 198 and accepted ¥525.8 billion. At the October auction, it received ¥3.139 trillion and accepted ¥537.1 billion.

Demand fell by much more than the amount sold in the competitive auction, and the average accepted yield rose 3.3 basis points to 3.756% from 3.723%.

Competitive coverage fell from 5.84× on Oct. 20 to 3.64× on Nov. 18 for Japan’s 20-year issue 198.

Source: Finance Ministry, Oct. 20 result · Finance Ministry, Nov. 18 result

At 5 p.m. in Tokyo, the midpoint of the Bank of Japan’s dollar-yen quote was 159.35 yen per dollar on Nov. 18, up one hundredth of a yen from 159.34 on Nov. 17.

Money-market broker Tokyo Tanshi reported a weighted average unsecured overnight call rate of 1.227%, unchanged from the previous day. The rate measures one-day borrowing in Japan’s call money market, for which the Bank of Japan publishes a daily series.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The currency and the overnight rate track different parts of a yen-funded trade.

The dollar-yen midpoint moved one sen between readings. The overnight call rate was unchanged.

FX figures are 5 p.m. snapshots. The overnight figure is a broker benchmark. The two readings do not identify funding terms or exposures for individual Bitcoin positions.

The exchange-rate figures are midpoints of the Bank of Japan’s 5 p.m. Tokyo dollar-yen quote ranges. The overnight rate is Tokyo Tanshi’s weighted average for Nov. 18, compared with its reported previous-day change.

FX figures are 5 p.m. snapshots. The overnight figure is a broker benchmark. The two readings do not identify funding terms or exposures for individual Bitcoin positions.

A trader borrowing yen short-term to hold Bitcoin faces higher costs to renew the loan if funding rates rise. If the trader sells Bitcoin for dollars and leaves the yen debt unhedged, a stronger yen makes repaying the loan in dollar terms more expensive.

Bank for International Settlements researchers documented that feedback during the August 2024 unwind in leveraged currency and equity trades.

Bitfinex reported that aggregate Bitcoin futures open interest fell to 645,750 BTC over Nov. 14 and 15, 8% below Nov. 11. The futures total shows how much exposure was outstanding but gives no breakdown by funding currency.

BIS research showed that although it identified $271 billion in yen-denominated bank loans to non-bank borrowers outside Japan in the first quarter of 2024, it could not determine how much was used for carry trades.

The Finance Ministry plans to offer about ¥300 billion at the Nov. 25 reopening of a 40-year bond, the next test of long-dated demand.

A stronger signal for Bitcoin would be rising short-term yen borrowing costs or a sustained strengthening of the yen, alongside shrinking futures exposure and evidence that traders cutting positions had yen liabilities.

Siri Modular Apple Watch face gains two new styles with watchOS 27.3

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Siri Modular, the new Apple Watch face in watchOS 27, is gaining new style options. watchOS 27.3, currently in beta, adds two more style options to the existing set. This gives users five options for adjusting what type of information appears on Siri Modular.

Siri Modular originally shipped with three style options. Each supports a small line complication above the time.

Additionally, the original three styles include these options:

In watchOS 27.3, Apple introduces two new Siri features that work with Apple Watch Series 12 and Apple Watch Ultra 4.

First, there’s Siri Recap which uses the Apple Watch to turn audio into high-level text summaries. Second, there’s Live Rewind which lets you view a transcript of the last 15 seconds of spoken word.

The two new Siri Modular Apple Watch face styles help surface three things: invoking Siri, viewing conversations with Siri, and viewing Siri Recaps on the Apple Watch.

The new styles include a pill-shaped module that includes icon shortcuts that go directly to Siri conversations, invoke Siri AI, or go directly to Siri Recaps in the Siri app.

One style features a large digital clock with the new three-button Siri module. Another includes a compact digital clock with a Smart Stack tile and the new three-button Siri module.

This brings the style options to five. If you use the three circular complications configuration, you can also assign a Siri icon that invokes Siri AI.

watchOS 27.3 is currently in beta and expected to be released officially in December.

Van Gogh painting sells for record $190.4m after row over its Nazi history

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sotheby's staff handle the "ChÃ¢taigniers en fleurs" painting by Vincent van Gogh at an auction preview in Hong Kong last month

Auction house Sotheby's has sold a painting by Vincent van Gogh for a record $190.4m (Â£144.8m).

The sale of ChÃ¢taigniers en fleurs ("Chestnut Trees in Blossom") shatters the renowned artist's previous auction record of $117.2 million set in 2022.

The historic sale in New York on Wednesday comes after an eleventh-hour agreement was reached to resolve a dispute regarding claims the art piece was previously sold under duress during the rise of the Nazis.

ChÃ¢taigniers en fleurs was painted in 1890 in the final two months of Van Gogh's life. It is considered one of the most important works of art from that period to remain in private hands, according to Sotheby's.

Sotheby's auction of $180m Van Gogh challenged over claims it was 'sold under duress'

It was not immediately clear who purchased the painting. The BBC has contacted Sotheby's for comment.

The oil painting, which was estimated to carry a value of $180m (Â£136m) prior to the sale, was put up for auction as part of a set called the Blaquier-Arrieta Collection.

Sotheby's announced in the hours before the auction that it had resolved a dispute between the Blaquier-Arrieta family and the heirs of the Fischer family - the prior German owners of the work - regarding the sale.

Representatives acting for heirs of the Fischer family had claimed the painting may have been sold under duress during the rise of the Nazis.

A lawyer for the family's heirs reportedly wrote to Sotheby's on their behalf, telling the auction house that the painting was sold in Amsterdam before World War Two because of financial losses the Fischer family's publishing company suffered under the Nazis. The BBC has contacted the lawyer for comment.

"In recognition of the painting's complex Nazi-era history and the suffering endured by the Fischer family during the Holocaust, the two families have reached a mutually satisfactory resolution and the work will be sold pursuant to their agreement," Sotheby's said on Wednesday.

Sotheby's website details the provenance of the oil painting and how it has researched the artwork changing hands since 1890.

It says Samuel Fischer - a famous German publisher - acquired it 1909 before it was passed down in his family after his death in 1934.

The painting was deposited with Paul Cassirer in Amsterdam in 1932 or 1933 before it went to Henriette and Marcel Kapferer in Paris in 1936, Sotheby's said.

The Blaquier family acquired it in 1975 and owned it for more than half a century before the latest sale, the auction house said.

How GTA 6 Is Leaning Into Advanced Physics For More Realistic Car Crashes

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Has this crazy timeline finally found balance? We can't know, but "Grand Theft Auto 6" is actually an honest-to-goodness playable game now. Sure, the longtime PC-only "BeamNG" became available on PlayStation 5 a month before GTA 6, but those "We got X before GTA 6" jokes can finally be put to rest. Thanks for 13 years of meme-ories.

With GTA 6, you'll become the ultimate Florida Man, as the gameplay actually extends beyond Miami-esque Vice City and into Rockstar Games' equally fantastic renderings of the Everglades, the Florida Keys, and elsewhere. For something escapist, though, GTA 6 is unapologetically realistic. Not only do vehicles in GTA 6 require fill-ups, your characters will even be tracked via Flock-like cameras. Factor in the realistic-looking crashes and you might just end up forgetting you're in-game and calling 911. Okay, maybe it's not quite that immersive, but the much-improved physics highlighted in vehicle collisions certainly add to the overall hyper-realistic visual effects.

There's no shortage of car racing games and driving simulators available at our fingertips. But as fun as, say, the Mario Kart franchise is, racing as a cow while riding a boombox with wheels is hardly ideal, let alone realistic. Don't even bother asking about crumple zones. Showcasing soft-body-style dynamics, however, adds so much more to the gameplay and is an impressive feat all its own.

When it comes to simulations, particularly collisions, how the vehicles crash and come apart generally falls into two categories: rigid body and soft body. As the names suggest, rigid bodies don't deform or change shape on-screen, no matter how severe the impact. With soft body physics, the causes and effects are shown. Sheet metal is compressed and contorts in a collision, for example.

With driving games, "BeamNG" was at the forefront and even shares a simple explanation of how it developed its soft-body physics simulation software model. For GTA 6, the driving dynamics and subsequent physics are previewed in the video game's trailers, especially in the mature-content extended version. While they may not be the same soft-body physics that "BeamNG" uses, the visual effects come close.

During chase scenes, bullets damage vehicles as they would in real life. You'll see tires blowing out, bullets ricocheting off grilles, and holes left in door panels. In other scenes, car windows actually shatter when smashed, fuel tanks rupture, and body panels deform. Other notable visual effects include functional dash gauges as well as dust and dirt that kick up as cars pull away.

Most impressive is that all of this is happening in real time during actual gameplay — these scenes aren't all preset. GTA BOOM reported that former Rockstar Games animator Mike York described how two different simulator systems can operate simultaneously through Rockstar's RAGE engine. Movements, impacts, and damage are calculated as they happen while thousands of physics computations per second occur in the background.

It's the little things that you might not have noticed, but now can appreciate. But did we have to wait so long? Well, at least we're not having to reenlist in the Army.

Why Bullish Stock Crushed it on Wednesday

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It topped analyst estimates in its latest reported quarter.

By Eric Volkman – Nov 18, 2026 at 7:43PM EST

The investing world was very bullish on cryptocurrency exchange operator Bullish (BLSH +5.66%) on Hump Day. That's mainly because the company posted an earnings report featuring a massive leap in headline profitability. Mr. Market rewarded this by sending Bullish's shares up by almost 6% in that trading session.

Before market open, Bullish, perhaps best known for owning the popular crypto news and analysis web portal CoinDesk, reported that its non-IFRS (adjusted) revenue in the third quarter was $84.4 million. That was up a sturdy 10% year over year, and was well above the average analyst estimate of $79.3 million.

On the bottom line, the company's net income swelled to over $576 million ($3.57 per share) from $18.5 million in the third quarter of 2025.

It was the vagaries of the crypto market that juiced Bullish's profitability. The value of its proprietary digital asset holdings flipped from an $11.4 million loss in the year-ago quarter to a $598.4 million gain. Stripping that and other one-time and unique items out of the equation, the company's adjusted net profit still recorded a gain, coming in at $18.7 million ($0.12 per share) from $13.8 million. Analysts were modeling $0.08 per share for the metric.

In the earnings release, Bullish announced the launch of a $300 million share repurchase program. It did not specify an end date for the initiative.

In the release, CEO Tom Farley signaled Bullish's strategy to grow its business via its pending acquisition. In May, the company agreed to acquire shareholder services company Equiniti in a deal valued at $4.2 billion; the transaction is expected to close this coming January. Farley aims to combine that company’s sprawling public-company shareholder registry with Bullish’s platform to offer tokenized stock trading.

I like that Bullish is active and assertive in building out its operations, and it's admirable that management is seeking ways to distinguish it in the crypto services sphere. That said, I'm not convinced tokenized securities will be a powerful motor of growth, so I'd temper my enthusiasm about its third-quarter showing.

Microsoft CEO Satya Nadella explains why he asked 60K employees to quit

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Microsoft CEO Satya Nadella explained Wednesday why he offered buyouts to 60,000 of his employees in October, essentially urging them to voluntarily quit instead of reducing the workforce via another massive layoff.

Short answer: AI, but not, he insists, in the way you might expect. He’s not saying he’s replacing employees with AI, especially software engineers. He’s saying he’s reorganizing Microsoft into a company where everyone uses AI to do their work, and their jobs become focused on managing that AI.

The buyout was “to make sure that at least everyone felt like they had agency in making choices,” he told Andrew Ross Sorkin during the DealBook Summit.

“Let’s take software engineering. We went from, ‘Well, we don’t buy that AI can ever do software.’ To suddenly, ‘Wow, it can do all software, and why do we need software engineers?’ To now rediscovering that software engineering is very much alive and, in fact, is going to thrive, except the way the team is organized and the work that a software engineer does has now got a new operating point,” he explained.

For some people, “that reorganization and change is sort of hard, right?” he added, “but, I mean, it has to be done.”

He offered the example of the team that builds Cowork, the AI agent in Microsoft 365 apps. He said the team consists of just 11 developers who have mastered using AI to do their jobs. “They build a software factory that builds the product. They don’t build the product.”

He believes that every employee will one day similarly become a manager of agents, rather than a person directly doing the work. “That level of direction is what, by the way, is going to happen in all jobs.”

But Microsoft, as a 51-year-old company, didn’t grow up as an AI-first company. “So the question is, how quickly can an incumbent transform the work, the workflow, and the work artifact, and with all the wage support that goes with it?” he said. “It’s going to take a bit more time than anyone expects. And there is going to be in all of this, you know, people who may say, ‘Hey, I have some other choices I want to make.’”

When asked about this same topic — why ask 60,000 people to voluntarily resign — last week by Gergely Orosz on his The Pragmatic Engineer podcast, Nadella further explained, “We have created enormous anxiety in people sort of feeling that they don’t have control of their own destiny because the way we have talked about this AI revolution.”

He called out talk about the singularity (the hypothetical point at which AI surpasses human intelligence) and predictions that AI will take away all jobs. “All of that leads anyone who even wants to change to be sort of anxious … You have to sort of walk the hard walk of really making the structural changes, getting people slotted, giving them opportunity.”

In other words, the buyout was the message to employees: You can get with the new AI-first program, which will radically change your job, or you choose to get on with your life. Elsewhere.

Elon Musk says: ‘I’m not MAGA’

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Elon Musk, who spent $277 million to get Donald Trump reelected president in 2024 and who, during that campaign, declared himself “dark MAGA” and who, after the election, was subsequently photographed sporting a cartoonishly large MAGA hat while visiting the Oval Office, has some news for Americans: None of that necessarily makes him MAGA.

“I’m not MAGA,” Musk declared Wednesday during a conversation with Andrew Ross Sorkin at The New York Times DealBook Summit. Musk joined the conversation via video call, as he is currently in China, joining President Trump for the APEC summit.

“Well, at least I’m not like … I’m not sure what MAGA means,” he backtracked. “If that means sort of blind adherence to a set of policies by the Republican Party, then I certainly don’t have that.”

The comments were made amidst a broader question about how Musk felt in the wake of this year’s midterms, during which a “blue wave” led to Democratic victories throughout the country. Musk, notably, spent a gargantuan amount of money (reportedly some $445 million) to support Republican candidates. Musk admitted Wednesday that such vast political spending might not have been a good use of his money.

“I feel pretty foolish about having spent so much money and not seen much for it,” he said. “Or maybe it would have been much worse without that. But it certainly shows that, you know, American elections are not just about money.”

During his conversation with Sorkin, Musk also seemed to lean away from his viral edgelord persona and claimed that he held no extreme views. He said: “I don’t think I have like, you know, any kind of extreme political views.”

Sorkin pushed Musk on this a little, noting that some people found his views on immigration extreme, to which Musk said: “Like what?” Mild laughter spread throughout the audience.

Ironically, the last time Musk spoke at DealBook, in 2023, he was in the middle of a very public battle with his social media site X’s advertisers. Critics had accused Musk of platforming a typhoon of hateful content on his site, and advertisers were threatening to walk over it. In response, Musk famously told Sorkin that he hoped those advertisers would “go f—” themselves.

Now Musk has an entirely new set of concerns. Democratic lawmakers in various committees have signaled that they may investigate Musk and his business activities as they probe potential corruption throughout the Trump administration.

Specifically, officials have said they’re interested in scrutinizing Musk’s role in DOGE, the “government efficiency” initiative that Musk launched inside the White House at the start of Trump’s new term, as well as Starlink’s activities in Ukraine, where the company has been accused of throttling the military’s access to its internet service.

Sorkin broached some of the potential concerns for the Tesla CEO: “You hear people like the chairman of the oversight committee saying, ‘Lawyer up,’ and you hear the president saying, ‘If I have to give him a pardon’ — meaning you — ‘I’ll give him a pardon.’”

Musk replied: “I don’t think I need a pardon by the way, because I haven’t committed any crimes.”

Musk then made one of those controversial statements he’s known for, appearing to compare an incoming Texas official to Lavrentiy Beria, the top head of the Soviet secret police under Josef Stalin.

“The incoming Texas, you know, House Majority Leader, said that I should go to prison,” Musk said. “I’m like, the guy’s trying to, you know channel Beria from, like, Stalin, where it’s, you know, show me the man, I’ll show you the crime type of thing.”

It’s not clear who (or what) Musk was talking about. Texas doesn’t have a House Majority Leader. However, he may have been referencing Gene Wu, who is the incoming chair of the Texas House Democratic caucus and who has suggested that Musk be investigated for potential criminal activity.

“Hopefully, in this country, you still need to do an actual crime before you go to prison,” Musk added.

Fathom Urges Rapid Implementation of California’s AI Oversight Plan

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

AI governance group Fathom said on November 18, 2026 that Governor Gavin Newsom’s new frontier AI roadmap reflected many of its recommendations, and urged California to implement mandatory independent evaluations for catastrophic risks quickly.

Fathom developed and advocated the independent verification organization (IVO) model, under which outside evaluators assess AI models and systems for risk. CEO and co-founder Andrew Freedman described the report as a concrete plan for putting independent oversight into practice and urged movement with “all deliberate speed.” He listed legislation, rulemaking, state-versus-federal authority, logistics, timing and costs as unresolved questions, and said Fathom would press state and federal governments for transparency, mandatory IVO risk measurement and government-enforced guardrails.

The California Government Operations Agency’s (GovOps) report, dated November 16, 2026 and released publicly on November 18, recommends legislative changes rather than imposing requirements itself. Its broader package includes baseline standards, emergency shutdown authority and possible limits on recursive self-improvement, building on SB 813’s IVO framework and AB 1405’s AI-auditor registry.

Its central oversight recommendation would require every large frontier AI developer to undergo continuing reviews by licensed IVOs, replacing the current voluntary approach for those developers. GovOps would license and continuously supervise the evaluators, with authority to revoke licenses when they lack sufficient expertise, independence or rigor. IVOs would submit unredacted assessments to the state, which would decide whether corrective action was warranted after engaging with the developer.

On both the division of responsibility and the legal test, Fathom says the report tracks its October 30, 2026 implementation memo. California would define acceptable safety outcomes in law, while IVOs would develop and update the technical methods and tests used to assess compliance. The proposed standard asks whether a frontier model is “unlikely to increase the risk of catastrophic harm above the level that would exist absent the model” and whether its anticipated benefits outweigh the residual risk. The design is meant to let testing methods change with the technology while preserving the state’s statutory outcome.

Recursive self-improvement (RSI), the point at which automated AI development becomes self-sustaining, is another area of overlap. The report would make verification of automated AI research and development metrics and assessment of the growing risks an initial IVO responsibility. Fathom’s memo also sought scrutiny of internal and external use, with pre-registered capability thresholds triggering state notification and a hold on further internal scaling pending reassessment.

The memo proposes phasing in each mandate once GovOps determines that designated evaluators have sufficient capacity and expertise in the relevant risk area, arguing that a dependable expectation of mandatory work would attract the capital and talent needed to expand independent verification instead of waiting for a mature market across every risk.

Initially, the state would assign an IVO from a predesignated pool, while the developer paid directly on a time-and-materials basis at state-benchmarked rates. Developer choice would expand in later phases as the pool deepened, but the state would retain powers to veto selections, require rotation, cap concentration, appoint additional assessors and decertify evaluators that lack rigor or independence. Payments would not depend on an assessment’s outcome.

Embedded evaluators would receive continuous access to necessary records, personnel and systems, including access for physical presence at defined decision points, backed by statutory confidentiality and anti-retaliation protections. Enforcement would remain with the state: the Attorney General could seek injunctions and civil penalties, while IVOs would make findings, prescribe corrective measures and refer imminent risks rather than seeking injunctions themselves or making their findings self-executing.

The practical test now sits with the Legislature and GovOps: converting the recommendations into binding duties while developing enough licensed evaluators and preserving credible state enforcement. Fathom’s design makes those steps interdependent, because each mandate depends on available expertise and an IVO’s findings require state action before they can carry coercive consequences.

Did Christopher Nolan Just Bring 4K Blu-Ray Discs Back to Stores?

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

The Odyssey hit the shelves of Best Buy, just in time for the holidays.

Physical media lovers, take note: Best Buy has begun selling 4K Ultra HD Blu-ray discs in-store and online. If you’ve been paying attention, you’ll know how big a pivot this is for the electronics retailer. Back in 2023, the company announced it would cease all sales of DVDs and Blu-rays entirely.

Now, thanks to Christopher Nolan’s blockbuster phenom and Oscar frontrunner The Odyssey, which is the first-ever movie to be filmed entirely on IMAX cameras, the company is once again stocking its shelves with Blu-rays. Right now, you’ll find only copies of the Greek epic in store (with a small selection of other titles available for purchase directly through the retailer’s online store), and that makes sense considering how popular it was in theaters and how awesome it will look on big TVs at home.

But this move also raises the question: Will other 4K and standard Blu-rays be coming to Best Buy stores in the near future?

A Best Buy spokesperson didn’t immediately reply to our request for comment.

When the retailer first announced it would be ditching DVD and Blu-ray sales, it cited the decades-long decline in physical media sales — which fell from its peak revenue benchmark of $16 billion in 2005 to less than $1 billion in 2024 — and planned to populate the store space with other tech-based products.

The Odyssey, the nearly 3-hour-long big-screen adaptation of Homer’s epic poem, follows King Odysseus (played by Matt Damon) as he leads his soldiers on a 10-year journey home after the Trojan War. It features a stacked slate of acting talent from Anne Hathaway and Tom Holland to Zendaya, Jon Bernthal and Charlize Theron, and earned $1.8 billion at the global box office.

Those details, along with the timing of the coming holiday shopping season, have apparently inspired Best Buy to change course.

It should be noted that physical media still has value for many people. In an era when movies and TV shows (purchased or otherwise) are suddenly pulled from streamers, the allure of owning titles on DVD and Blu-ray endures. Not to mention, 4K Blu-rays offer better picture quality than their compressed, streaming versions.

Google’s AI Controls Have Major Districts Rethinking Search

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The New York Times reported on November 18, 2026, that New York City, Los Angeles and Chicago school officials are considering replacements for Google Search because Google’s coming school controls tie Search access to its AI features, in a report carried by The Verge.

The control change due in 2027 links the two settings: a district that turns off Gemini-powered features will turn off Search as well. Google education vice president Chris Phillips said in comments carried by the report that Search and AI are deeply integrated and that schools turning off the AI features would not retain access.

New York City schools chancellor Kamar H. Samuels said in a statement carried by the report: “If Google doesn’t reconsider their position, we will explore other search options.” Chicago Public Schools also said it would consider other providers if Google could not supply Search without AI.

Google’s announcement on November 3, 2026 set out the control changes and promised more details before rollout. Under the plan, K-12 student accounts will have Gemini, Gemini Notebook, Gemini in Classroom and Google Search switched off unless administrators explicitly enable them.

In a statement to The Verge, Google spokesperson Laura Breen described the 2027 controls as a response to educator feedback and said Google hears concerns about students using AI to shortcut learning while sharing the goal of helping students learn.

Google’s educational case for the integration rests on Guided Learning, which the November 3 announcement describes as a way to keep students actively challenged without overwhelming them. It uses open-ended questions and step-by-step breakdowns rather than immediately supplying final answers.

When Search identifies certain kinds of homework queries, it will prompt students to reason through the material step by step rather than display a solution outright. Google said it would pilot the Search version in select U.S. schools later in November 2026 to gather educator feedback before expanding to other districts over the following months.

Guided Learning also became globally available in Google Classroom that day. Activities there are anchored in teacher-assigned class material, and teachers receive real-time insights to identify where a class may need extra support.

In a release issued November 13, 2026, New York City Council Speaker Julie Menin and Council Member Shekar Krishnan said they had written to Google CEO Sundar Pichai and requested written answers by November 19, 2026. Krishnan chairs the council’s Committee on Oversight and Investigations.

Mayor Zohran Mamdani announced a one-year moratorium in September 2026 on AI use by public-school students from 2-K through eighth grade. The council said Google’s guidance puts those students at risk of losing Search, turning an AI restriction into a choice about access to a basic research tool.

The letter asks whether students will retain Search without opting into AI Mode, AI Overviews or any student-facing generative AI, and why Google cannot offer a student version that excludes those features. It points to YouTube Kids as an example of Google modifying a product for younger users through different features and content controls.

It also asks what safeguards would preserve genuine district choice and how Google measures, monitors and mitigates AI-generated misinformation in educational settings. On privacy, the council asks whether Gemini and other classroom AI products collect student data, what the data is used for, where it is stored and whether Google earns revenue from selling or using it.

Google’s AI in Education report says schools retain ownership of their data and that, within core Workspace for Education services, student data is not sold, used for advertising or used to train generative AI models. That policy’s stated scope is core Workspace services, while the council’s request explicitly names Gemini and other classroom AI products.

For district leaders, the dispute turns an administrative AI setting into a question of student access to research. The report also noted that U.S. schools use Google Classroom, Chromebooks and other services extensively, making a complete break with the company potentially difficult. A new search provider could address one service, but any wider separation would involve far more than replacing a search box.

What is Ethereum actually for? A judge and Vitalik Buterin both left it open

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

the 16th of November, a federal judge acquitted Anton and James Peraire-Bueno in the “first of its kind” wire fraud prosecution over a $25 million Ethereum exploit. This judgement came about after the government failed to prove the brothers made any “material misrepresentation.”

The ruling, filed in the Southern District of New York (24-CR-293), came the same day as when Bitwise CEO Hunter Horsley pitched stablecoins as a backup for banks.

On a similar note, Ethereum [ETH] co-founder Vitalik Buterin argued that digital assets do not match up to investing in houses. These three developments, when taken together, compound the question of what crypto is actually for.

On the 2nd of April 2023, the brothers seemingly exploited a bug in the MEV-Boost relay software, due to which they could view transactions that sandwich traders believed were private.

The brothers’ alleged proposal for replacement transactions, in turn, resulted in massive losses of roughly $25 million within seconds.

Finally, on the 16th of November 2026, Judge Jessica G. L. Clarke, when granting an acquittal, wrote,

The question before the Court is not whether the Peraire-Buenos’ conduct was right or moral, or even whether it violated uncharged crimes.

It is simply whether the evidence at trial, construed in a light most favorable to the Government, could support wire fraud and related convictions in this case. Because the Government failed to prove that the Peraire-Buenos made any material misrepresentations in the execution of their scheme, the motion is granted.

The court analyzed the government’s theories – including the “lure transactions” and the so-called “false signature,” and found nothing that could support a wire fraud conviction.

A couple of days later, on the 18th of November, Horsley posted on X:

Rolling bank outages in the AI era – Consider having some stablecoins or crypto just in case.

The post came as thousands of Chase, Bank of America, and Wells Fargo customers reported being unable to access their accounts.

Vitalik Buterin, in a separate post on November 18, argued that younger generations have been investing in digital assets over real estate options; doing so, he noted, reduces –

Perverse incentives that drive people to push to keep housing expensive.

But he added a condition: housing had the advantage of being –

A culture and policy-encouraged default of investing in a safe non-crazy thing that you can’t easily liquidate.

Modern platforms encourage the opposite of this, leading to more irresponsible behavior… We need the equivalent of those good properties that housing investment has, for modern digital assets.

The three developments have been framing crypto’s developing role separately, but these assets have a long way to go before they can fully replace TradFi.

Vitalik Buterin himself leaves the question open, as he observed,

We need the equivalent of those good properties that housing investment has, for modern digital assets.

The ruling did not answer that question, nor did the outage.
